 for s in c['segments']:
  cls='prose' if s['speaker']=='N' else 'dialogue'
  # An EPUB reader does not read speaker metadata. Producers should use timeline for exact casting.
  attr='' if s['speaker']=='N' else ' data-speaker="'+escape(CAST[s['speaker']][0],quote=True)+'"'
  bits.append('<p class="'+cls+'"'+attr+'>'+escape(s['text'])+'</p>')
 pages['chap_'+c['num']+'.xhtml']=xmlpage(c['title'],'\n'.join(bits))
cover_html=xmlpage('Cover','<div class="cover"><img src="images/cover.jpg" alt="Earth-GK The City That Lied cover artwork"/></div>','cover')
title_html=xmlpage(TITLE,'''<div class="titlepage"><p class="title-eyebrow">GRIZZLY KNIGHTS · EARTH-GK</p><p class="book-title">THE CITY THAT LIED</p><p>An unofficial Marvel alternate-universe story</p><p>Volume One · Full-cast audio edition</p></div>''')
toc_links=''.join('<li><a href="chap_'+c['num']+'.xhtml">'+escape(c['title'])+'</a></li>' for c in chapters)

colophon=xmlpage('About This Edition','<h1>About This Edition</h1><p>This is an unofficial, original fan-fiction story using Marvel characters in a speculative alternate continuity, Earth-GK. It is not affiliated with, licensed by, or endorsed by Marvel Entertainment, Disney, or ElevenLabs.</p><p>The story treats superpowers and supervillains as real within the fiction. The human consequences and coping patterns are dramatized, not offered as clinical diagnoses.</p><p class="colophon">A separate full-cast performance script and speaker-timeline file provide director cues for audiobook production; the EPUB itself remains free from spoken metadata or artificial performance tags.</p>')
uid='urn:uuid:3bb54d77-cb48-4d1b-9ed7-8294e80c43ee'
items=[('cover','cover.xhtml','application/xhtml+xml',''),('title','title.xhtml','application/xhtml+xml',''),('nav','nav.xhtml','application/xhtml+xml','nav'),('about','about.xhtml','application/xhtml+xml',''),('css','style.css','text/css',''),('coverimg','images/cover.jpg','image/jpeg','cover-image'),('ncx','toc.ncx','application/x-dtbncx+xml','')]+[('ch'+c['num'],'chap_'+c['num']+'.xhtml','application/xhtml+xml','') for c in chapters]
manifest=''.join('<item id="'+id+'" href="'+href+'" media-type="'+kind+'"'+((' properties="'+p+'"') if p else '')+'/>' for id,href,kind,p in items)
spineids=['cover','title']+['ch'+c['num'] for c in chapters]+['about']
spine=''.join('<itemref idref="'+id+'"/>' for id in spineids)
modified=datetime(2026,10,28,23,0,tzinfo=timezone.utc).strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ')
opf='''<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?>
<package xmlns="http://www.idpf.org/2007/opf" version="3.0" unique-identifier="uid" xml:lang="en"><metadata xmlns:dc="http://purl.org/dc/elements/1.1/"><dc:identifier id="uid">'''+uid+'''</dc:identifier><dc:title>Earth-GK: The City That Lied</dc:title><dc:creator>Grizzly Knights / Earth-GK</dc:creator><dc:language>en</dc:language><dc:subject>Marvel fan fiction; Superhero fiction; Alternate universe; Psychological thriller</dc:subject><dc:description>Spider-Man, Black Cat, Mary Jane Watson, the Human Torch, and Deadpool confront Mysterio's city-sized performance and Kingpin's contracts.</dc:description><meta property="dcterms:modified">'''+modified+'''</meta></metadata><manifest>'''+manifest+'''</manifest><spine toc="ncx">'''+spine+'</spine></package>'''
container='''<?xml version="1.0" encoding="utf-8"?><container xmlns="urn:oasis:names:tc:opendocument:xmlns:container" version="1.0"><rootfiles><rootfile full-path="OEBPS/content.opf" media-type="application/oebps-package+xml"/></rootfiles></container>'''
navmap=''.join(f'<navPoint id="nav{i+1}" playOrder="{i+1}"><navLabel><text>{escape(c["title"])}</text></navLabel><content src="chap_{c["num"]}.xhtml"/></navPoint>' for i,c in enumerate(chapters))

 z.writestr('mimetype',b'application/epub+zip',compress_type=zipfile.ZIP_STORED)
 for name,content in [('META-INF/container.xml',container),('OEBPS/content.opf',opf),('OEBPS/toc.ncx',ncx),('OEBPS/style.css',css),('OEBPS/cover.xhtml',cover_html),('OEBPS/title.xhtml',title_html),('OEBPS/nav.xhtml',nav),('OEBPS/about.xhtml',colophon)]:
  z.writestr(name,content,compress_type=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED)
 z.write(cover,'OEBPS/images/cover.jpg',compress_type=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED)
 for name,content in pages.items():z.writestr('OEBPS/'+name,content,compress_type=zipfile.ZIP_DEFLATED)

# Exact speaker-by-speaker performance edition, with all director cues separated from clean EPUB
script=['# EARTH-GK: THE CITY THAT LIED — FULL-CAST PERFORMANCE SCRIPT','', 'Unofficial Marvel alternate-universe work · 11 sections · '+str(wc)+' words','', '**Director:** Speaker labels and bracketed acting directions are metadata, **not spoken dialogue**. Only text after the tag is spoken. Some tags are editorial guidance, not guaranteed ElevenLabs control tokens. Do not paste the entire script as unparsed text for single-voice narration. Use the accompanying structured timeline to render speaker chunks.','', '**Performance:** The jokes are behaviors, not diagnoses. Hold pauses where characters choose restraint. Beck wants an audience; Fisk wants control; the five leads want conflicting things they cannot easily name.','']
timeline=[]
for c in chapters:
 script+=['','## '+c['num']+' — '+c['title'],'']
 for i,s in enumerate(c['segments'],1):
  label='UATU / NARRATOR' if s['speaker']=='N' else CAST[s['speaker']][0]
  perf=(s['direction']+' ') if s['direction'] else ''
  spoken=s['text'] if s['speaker']=='N' else s['text'][1:-1]
  script += ['**'+label+'**','',perf+spoken,'']
  timeline.append({'chapter':int(c['num']),'chapter_title':c['title'],'sequence':i,'speaker_id':s['speaker'],'speaker_name':label,'acting_direction':s['direction'],'spoken_text':spoken})

from pathlib import Path
from mutagen.id3 import ID3,TIT2,TPE1,TALB,TCON,COMM,APIC,CHAP,CTOC,TDRC
from mutagen.mp3 import MP3
import wave, tempfile, subprocess, json, os, time
O=Path('/mnt/data');W=Path('/mnt/data/earth_gk_rewrite')
P='Earth_GK_The_City_That_Lied'
timeline=json.loads((O/(P+'_Full_Cast_Timeline.json')).read_text(encoding='utf8'))['segments']
voices={
'N':('en-us+m2',159,35),
'PETER':('en-us+m3',176,51),
'MJ':('en-us+f3',172,64),
'FELICIA':('en-us+f2',152,45),
'JOHNNY':('en-us+m4',187,55),
'WADE':('en-us+m5',193,28),
'BECK':('en-us+m7',161,50),
'FISK':('en-us+m1',136,25),
'RHINO':('en-us+m1',115,20),
'REED':('en-us+m6',161,46),
'SUE':('en-us+f4',149,56),
'BEN':('en-us+m1',134,28),
'VALE':('en-us+f3',154,42),
'BRUNO':('en-us+m6',166,40),
'REPORTER':('en-us+m4',169,55),
'CURATOR':('en-us+m6',150,55),
'BOY':('en-us+f5',165,67),
'GUARD':('en-us+m3',169,40),
'DISPATCH':('en-us+f1',150,42),
'BEN_PARKER':('en-us+m2',131,35),
'JONAH':('en-us+m1',189,65),
}
raw=W/'revised_scratch.wav'
frames=0;rate=22050;marks=[]
start=time.time()
with wave.open(str(raw),'wb') as out:
 out.setnchannels(1);out.setsampwidth(2);out.setframerate(rate)
 def silence(seconds):
  global frames
  count=int(rate*seconds);out.writeframes(b'\0\0'*count);frames+=count
 def say(text,speaker):
  global frames
  text=text.replace('\u2014',' - ').replace('\u2013',' - ').replace('Earth-GK','Earth G K').replace('City Mirror','City Mirror')
  voice,speed,pitch=voices.get(speaker,voices['N'])
  with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix='.wav',delete=False) as t:temp=t.name
  try:
   subprocess.run(['espeak','-v',voice,'-s',str(speed),'-p',str(pitch),'-a','170','-w',temp,text],stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL,stderr=subprocess.PIPE,check=True,timeout=25)
   with wave.open(temp,'rb') as inp:
    assert (inp.getnchannels(),inp.getsampwidth(),inp.getframerate())==(1,2,rate)
    while True:
     chunk=inp.readframes(rate*10)
     if not chunk:break
     out.writeframes(chunk);frames+=len(chunk)//2
  finally:
   os.unlink(temp)
 say('Grizzly Knights. Earth G K. Volume One. The City That Lied. An unofficial alternate universe Marvel story.','N')
 silence(.9)
 ch_last=-1
 for i,s in enumerate(timeline):
  chapter=s['chapter']
  if chapter!=ch_last:
   if ch_last>=0:silence(1.25)
   title=('Prologue. ' if chapter==0 else f'Chapter {chapter}. ')+s['chapter_title']
   marks.append({'index':chapter,'title':title,'start_ms':int(frames*1000/rate)})
   say(title,'N');silence(.5);ch_last=chapter
  say(s['spoken_text'],s['speaker_id'])
  silence(.18 if s['speaker_id']=='N' else .26)
  if (i+1)%50==0:print(f'Rendered {i+1}/{len(timeline)} segments; {frames/rate/60:.1f} minutes of speech; elapsed {time.time()-start:.1f}s',flush=True)
 silence(1.1)
 length_ms=int(frames*1000/rate)
for i,ch in enumerate(marks):ch['end_ms']=marks[i+1]['start_ms'] if i+1<len(marks) else length_ms
assert len(marks)==11
print('WAV done:',round(length_ms/60000,1),'min /',round(raw.stat().st_size/1e6,1),'MB. Encoding MP3...',flush=True)
mp3=O/(P+'_Scratch_Audiobook.mp3')
subprocess.run(['ffmpeg','-hide_banner','-loglevel','error','-y','-threads','2','-i',str(raw),'-c:a','libmp3lame','-b:a','48k','-ar','24000','-ac','1','-id3v2_version','3',str(mp3)],check=True,timeout=240)
raw.unlink()
# Populate ID3. This is a scratch narrator/cast reference, not a produced ElevenLabs audioplay.
tags=ID3(mp3)
tags.add(TIT2(encoding=3,text=['Earth-GK: The City That Lied — Scratch Full-Cast Audiobook']))
tags.add(TPE1(encoding=3,text=['Grizzly Knights / Earth-GK']))
tags.add(TALB(encoding=3,text=['The City That Lied — Volume One']))
tags.add(TCON(encoding=3,text=['Audiobook / Superhero Fiction']))
tags.add(TDRC(encoding=3,text=['2026']))
tags.add(COMM(encoding=3,lang='eng',desc='Production status',text=['Local mechanical multi-voice reference recording. NOT an ElevenLabs rendering or professional acted voice track.'] ))
tags.add(APIC(encoding=3,mime='image/jpeg',type=3,desc='Cover artwork',data=(O/(P+'_Cover.jpg')).read_bytes()))
children=[]
for chapter in marks:
 ident=f'earthgk-citylied-{chapter["index"]:02d}'
 children.append(ident)
 tags.add(CHAP(element_id=ident,start_time=chapter['start_ms'],end_time=chapter['end_ms'],start_offset=0xFFFFFFFF,end_offset=0xFFFFFFFF,sub_frames=[TIT2(encoding=3,text=[chapter['title']])]))
tags.add(CTOC(element_id='toc',flags=3,child_element_ids=children,sub_frames=[TIT2(encoding=3,text=['Contents'])]))
tags.save(mp3,v2_version=3)
a=MP3(mp3)
assert a.info.length>3000 and a.info.length<6500,('Unexpected recording runtime',a.info.length)
assert len(ID3(mp3).getall('CHAP'))==11
assert len(ID3(mp3).getall('APIC'))==1
(O/(P+'_Chapter_Timings.json')).write_text(json.dumps({'runtime_seconds':round(a.info.length,2),'chapter_markers':marks},indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print('COMPLETE',mp3,'MINUTES',round(a.info.length/60,1),'SIZE MB',round(mp3.stat().st_size/1e6,2),'SEGMENTS',len(timeline),flush=True)

  silence(.18 if s['speaker_id']=='N' else .26)
  if (i+1)%50==0:print(f'Rendered {i+1}/{len(timeline)} segments; {frames/rate/60:.1f} minutes of speech; elapsed {time.time()-start:.1f}s',flush=True)
 silence(1.1)
 length_ms=int(frames*1000/rate)
for i,ch in enumerate(marks):ch['end_ms']=marks[i+1]['start_ms'] if i+1<len(marks) else length_ms
assert len(marks)==11
print('WAV done:',round(length_ms/60000,1),'min /',round(raw.stat().st_size/1e6,1),'MB. Encoding MP3...',flush=True)
mp3=O/(P+'_Scratch_Audiobook.mp3')
subprocess.run(['ffmpeg','-hide_banner','-loglevel','error','-y','-threads','2','-i',str(raw),'-c:a','libmp3lame','-b:a','48k','-ar','24000','-ac','1','-id3v2_version','3',str(mp3)],check=True,timeout=240)
raw.unlink()
tags=ID3(mp3)
tags.add(TIT2(encoding=3,text=['Earth-GK: The City That Lied — Scratch Full-Cast Audiobook']))
tags.add(TPE1(encoding=3,text=['Grizzly Knights / Earth-GK']))
tags.add(TALB(encoding=3,text=['The City That Lied — Volume One']))
tags.add(TCON(encoding=3,text=['Audiobook / Superhero Fiction']))
tags.add(TDRC(encoding=3,text=['2026']))
tags.add(COMM(encoding=3,lang='eng',desc='Production status',text=['Local mechanical multi-voice reference recording. NOT an ElevenLabs rendering or professional acted voice track.']))
tags.add(APIC(encoding=3,mime='image/jpeg',type=3,desc='Cover artwork',data=(O/(P+'_Cover.jpg')).read_bytes()))
children=[]
for chapter in marks:
 ident=f'earthgk-citylied-{chapter["index"]:02d}'
 children.append(ident)
 tags.add(CHAP(element_id=ident,start_time=chapter['start_ms'],end_time=chapter['end_ms'],start_offset=0xFFFFFFFF,end_offset=0xFFFFFFFF,sub_frames=[TIT2(encoding=3,text=[chapter['title']])]))
tags.add(CTOC(element_id='toc',flags=3,child_element_ids=children,sub_frames=[TIT2(encoding=3,text=['Contents'])]))
tags.save(mp3,v2_version=3)
a=MP3(mp3)
assert a.info.length>3000 and a.info.length<6500,('Unexpected recording runtime',a.info.length)
assert len(ID3(mp3).getall('CHAP'))==11
assert len(ID3(mp3).getall('APIC'))==1
(O/(P+'_Chapter_Timings.json')).write_text(json.dumps({'runtime_seconds':round(a.info.length,2),'chapter_markers':marks},indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print('COMPLETE',mp3,'MINUTES',round(a.info.length/60,1),'SIZE MB',round(mp3.stat().st_size/1e6,2),'SEGMENTS',len(timeline),flush=True)

from zipfile import ZipFile,ZIP_STORED,ZIP_DEFLATED

====================================================

FULL REPLACEMENT EDITION / VOLUME ONE
Unofficial Marvel alternate-universe fan fiction.

The prior Earth-GK deliverable, "The Last Good Shift", was an EMS drama,
not the Marvel story requested. This is a new 10,103-word superhero novella,
not a renamed or cosmetically edited copy of the previous file.

STORY CORE:
- Main leads: Spider-Man, Black Cat, Mary Jane Watson, Johnny Storm, Deadpool
- Antagonists: Mysterio, Wilson Fisk, Rhino
- Other Marvel players: Sue Storm, Reed Richards, Ben Grimm, J. Jonah Jameson

audit='''# Earth-GK — Revision audit / version 2

## The recurring failure being corrected
The creative constraint was **a Marvel universe populated by psychologically realistic people**, drawing from the experience of EMS, fire, law enforcement and military operators. The earlier delivered work made a non-Marvel emergency response scenario the center of the story. That is a premise substitution, not a minor tone error.

## Evidence of correction in the story

| Requirement | Concrete scene and causal contribution |
| --- | --- |
| Marvel villains originate the crisis | Quentin Beck's city-sized Mysterio illusions are financed through Wilson Fisk's contracts; Rhino transports the device in chapter 1 |
| Spider-Man is indispensable | Stops Rhino, distinguishes false images from physical hazards, and saves Beck at the cost of an easy evidence seizure in chapter 8 |
| Black Cat is indispensable | Steals the prism, navigates an emotionally targeted trap, and recovers the authentication records that would otherwise be destroyed |
| Mary Jane is not scenery | Identifies the manipulated video, confronts Fisk, opens the official alert channel and publicly preserves the evidence chain |
| Human Torch uses power with real constraints | Clears aerial projectors while protecting actual people and structures; remains stationary when it is the useful choice |
| Deadpool's behavior carries real consequences | Admits a previous contract killing, does not demand forgiveness, follows Dr. Vale's instructions rather than making a reckless heroic gesture |
| Marvel relationships matter | Peter/MJ emotional boundary, Felicia/Peter trust dispute, Sue/Johnny family bond, Ben/Johnny affection, Fisk/MJ power contest |
| Operational realism is character texture, not genre change | Humor under fear, self-endangerment, over-functioning, violence and restraint, aftermath, institutional records and public accountability |
| Complete story | Eleven sequential sections: inciting crime, regrouping, coordinated threat, showdown and aftermath |

## Production checks
- EPUB 3: required mimetype first and uncompressed, XML and XHTML validated, eleven chapters in navigation and spine, cover embedded.
- Audio scratch: 413 source-to-voice mapped segments, eleven ID3 chapters, entire story present; audio is locally synthesized and mechanical, not a performed ElevenLabs production.
- Clean EPUB remains readable without stage tags; detailed direction remains in separate files.
- The old EMS-centered novella and recording are not included in this new release.

## Boundaries
Psychological motivation is interpretation of fictional characters, not verified clinical diagnosis. The supplied Wade file had no substantive text. This is unofficial Marvel fan fiction, not a canonical story or licensed property.
'''
(O/(P+'_Revision_Audit.md')).write_text(audit,encoding='utf8')
paths=[

# EARTH-GK: THE CITY THAT LIED — FULL-CAST PERFORMANCE SCRIPT

Unofficial Marvel alternate-universe work · 11 sections · 10103 words

**Director:** Speaker labels and bracketed acting directions are metadata, **not spoken dialogue**. Only text after the tag is spoken. Some tags are editorial guidance, not guaranteed ElevenLabs control tokens. Do not paste the entire script as unparsed text for single-voice narration. Use the accompanying structured timeline to render speaker chunks.

**Performance:** The jokes are behaviors, not diagnoses. Hold pauses where characters choose restraint. Beck wants an audience; Fisk wants control; the five leads want conflicting things they cannot easily name.


## 00 — The Man in the Fishbowl

**UATU / NARRATOR**

Three nights before his promised Friday premiere, at 2:13 in the morning, Quentin Beck made the Statue of Liberty disappear.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

Not from the harbor. Not from history. Only from the eyes of eleven men standing inside a decommissioned subway station beneath Midtown, each of whom had been paid enough to sign a nondisclosure agreement and too little to ask what it meant.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

On the monitoring wall, Lady Liberty remained exactly where she belonged, green against the dark water. In the station, an apparently endless ocean rolled over the platform edge. A gull cried. Salt spray chilled the faces of men whose boots stood on dry concrete.

**Quentin Beck / Mysterio**

[soft, pleased] Look at that. They know the room is empty. Every instrument says the room is empty. And one of them is about to swim.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

The first guard dropped to his knees. The second gripped a column. The third ran toward a staircase that the projection had made look like open sky. The guard reached for a door that wasn't there, struck his shoulder against a real wall, and fell.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

Beck winced, not at the injury, but at the ruined composition.

**Quentin Beck / Mysterio**

[irritated] Too much saturation on the exit. A doorway should invite. That one threatened.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

He had spent years being called a man with a fishbowl on his head by a boy in red-and-blue pajamas. In fairness, the helmet was shaped like a fishbowl. Quentin had never objected to the observation. He objected to the suggestion that the shape was the interesting thing about it.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

A second screen displayed the underwriting company: FISK URBAN RENEWAL. Another showed the security contract: CITY MIRROR. Another carried an encrypted folder of behavioral histories, photographs, and conversations that no one had given him permission to own.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

Quentin selected a photograph of a brown-haired man with tired eyes.

**Quentin Beck / Mysterio**

[almost tender] Peter. You'll finally watch the picture I made for you.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

A broad man waited beyond the glass in a black suit that cost more than the station's electrical wiring. Wilson Fisk occupied a doorway the way most men occupied a room: completely, and with an expectation of apology from the architecture.

**Wilson Fisk / Kingpin**

[calm] I paid for a municipal demonstration. I was told there would be no injuries.

**Quentin Beck / Mysterio**

[dismissive] There were no injuries in the demonstration. There was one injury in the audience.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

Fisk's expression did not change. That was more frightening than a raised voice.

**Wilson Fisk / Kingpin**

[quiet] The difference is legally relevant. Morally, it is an excuse. Do not confuse my willingness to purchase an excuse with a willingness to hear one.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

Beck bowed his head, a movement a spectator might have mistaken for obedience. When Fisk left, Quentin enlarged the personnel photographs. Spider-Man. Black Cat. The Human Torch. Mary Jane Watson. Deadpool. A city full of cameras, and the five people most capable of spoiling the premiere.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

On the far screen, a recording of a woman screaming in a burning building played without sound. Quentin touched a slider marked PERSONAL RECOGNITION.

**Quentin Beck / Mysterio**

[a whisper] There. Now you have a part in the show.

**UATU / NARRATOR**

An observer who knew the future might have warned him that people were not characters. Quentin would have called that a note from someone who didn't understand theatre.

## Communications & Linguistic Profile

# UATU DOSSIER — FELICIA HARDY / BLACK CAT
## Section 6: Communications & Linguistic Profile

---

### 6.1 Baseline Voice

The subject's natural speaking voice — across forty years of canon and every writer who has handled her — sits in a narrow, identifiable band: **mid-tempo, low-affect, contralto-warm, smiling-through-the-line**. She rarely raises her voice. She rarely speaks quickly. The default delivery is the half-turned-away aside — a remark addressed nominally to the room but engineered to land on one specific person, who is meant to feel singled out by the privacy of it. She speaks to be overheard *by the right ear only*.

Sentence length is short to medium. She avoids the long-clause sentence; her grammar is tight, declarative, and almost entirely free of hedges ("I think," "maybe," "sort of" appear only when she is deliberately performing softness). The absence of hedging is a tell in itself — most speakers of her demographic and apparent affect use them as social lubricant; she does not need the lubrication because she is not seeking permission to speak. The grammar of someone who decided long ago she would never again wait to be invited.

Vocabulary is wider than she lets on. She codeswitches *downward* far more often than she codeswitches up — she will play to a fence's level, to a mark's level, to a thug's level. With Peter she occasionally lets a stray polysyllable or art-history reference slip and then half-buries it with a smirk, as if embarrassed to have shown the inventory. Empire State coursework, autodidactic reading in art and gemology, and a working museum-curator's vocabulary all live under the cat-burglar patter. The patter is the costume; the vocabulary is the face.

---

### 6.2 The Pet-Name System

The single most cited verbal habit in her file. She names people before she answers them. **"Lover," "sweetheart," "honey," "sugar," "darling," "kitten," "tiger,"** and — reserved almost exclusively for Peter and a small handful of equivalents — **"Spider."** The pet-name is not affection. It is a *frame-setting device*:

- It establishes that *she* is the one who gets to name.
- It collapses the interlocutor into a class of object (lover, kitten) that she controls the membership of.
- It pre-empts being named first, which would be a frame-loss.
- It functions as an affectionate **soft put-down** when deployed to strangers, equals, or adversaries.

The hierarchy is meaningful. "Spider" is reserved, intimate, and almost never said in front of others. "Lover" is the public-private hybrid — said in front of others *as if* private, to manufacture an audience-effect. "Sweetheart" and "sugar" are the workplace pet-names, deployed to fences, marks, henchmen, and police she is currently outrunning. "Kitten" is condescending and used on women she is dismissing. "Darling" is the high-society register, used when she is dressed up. She has never, in any era, used her own father's name without softening it; she has also never called Peter "Pete," which is data — "Pete" would be Mary Jane's register, and Felicia knows it.

When she stops using pet-names mid-conversation and switches to the actual proper noun — "Peter," cold — the temperature of the scene has changed and the partner has approximately one beat to recalibrate.

---

### 6.3 The Quip Architecture

She makes jokes constantly. The structure is consistent enough to map:

1. **The setup is the threat or the wound.** She does not riff out of nothing; she riffs *on* the dangerous content. A gun pointed at her produces a comment on the gun. A confession of feeling produces a comment on the confession.
2. **The punchline collapses the threat into a sexual or aesthetic register.** "Is that for me?" applied indiscriminately to weapons, wallets, and emotional vulnerability.
3. **The smile is held a beat longer than the joke earns.** This is the deflection lock — the held smile prevents the partner from advancing into the seriousness she just defused.

Quip frequency rises monotonically with emotional temperature. In low-stakes scenes she will go several panels without a wisecrack. In a confession scene — Peter trying to say something true — the quip density triples. **The quip rate is a thermometer for how exposed she feels.** When she goes quiet and stops quipping in a hot scene, the file flag is *acute*: she has either decided to be honest (rare) or is about to leave (common).

---

### 6.4 Diction — Recurrent Lexical Field

Her preferred vocabulary clusters in a few semantic fields and is remarkably stable across writers:

- **Felines:** purr, claws, scratch, pounce, nine lives, curiosity, prowl, stray. She works the metaphor knowingly; she also lives inside it. She will say "I purr" with no irony in private and full irony in public.
- **Gambling / luck:** odds, bet, deal, draw, fold, the house, lucky. The luck-powers period intensified this and it never fully receded. She talks about plans in terms of percentages and odds even when the powers are gone.
- **Gemology / art:** carat, facet, provenance, setting, original, fake, copy. She uses these as relational metaphors as often as material ones — "you're the real thing," "he's a copy," "the provenance is what makes it worth anything."
- **Theatrical:** scene, act, curtain, audience, encore, stage, costume, mask. She narrates her own life in produ

# 6. COMMUNICATIONS & LINGUISTIC PROFILE

*Six decades of recorded speech across thought-balloon, dialogue-balloon, panel caption, and intercepted radio/comm chatter. The subject is, by volume, among the most verbose costumed operators in the field — a fact which is itself diagnostic.*

---

## 6.1 BASELINE REGISTER AND IDIOLECT

The subject's native idiolect is **outer-borough mid-century American English, college-educated, scientifically inflected, with a persistent residue of working-class Queens cadence** that he has never made any observable effort to scrub. Vowel-flattening on words like "coffee," "talk," "Aunt"; intervocalic /t/ surviving in careful speech but going to a soft flap when tired; occasional "youse" surfacing in moments of inattention with old neighborhood contacts. The substrate is Forest Hills, not Manhattan, and the substrate shows whenever fatigue, fear, or familiarity disinhibits the surface management.

Lexically, his active vocabulary is **bimodally distributed**: a high-register technical register drawn from organic chemistry, polymer science, applied physics, mechanical engineering, and (post-Horizon/Parker Industries) materials science and nanofabrication; and a low-to-mid register of vernacular American comedy, sitcom rhythms, pulp-magazine and old-radio reference, sports patter (Mets-leaning, never Yankees, occasional Knicks self-pity), and a steady drip of pop-culture allusion that ages with him but never fully modernizes — he still reaches first for a Bogart line, a Honeymooners cadence, an old Universal-monster reference, a Twilight Zone framing, before he reaches for whatever is current. This generational lag in reference is real and consistent across writers: it reads as a man whose interior reference library was assembled in late-night TV reruns and library trips during a lonely adolescence, and who has never had the leisure to fully refresh it.

What is conspicuously **absent** from his idiolect is also diagnostic. He does not curse with any meaningful frequency. "Damn" and "hell" are his ceiling in extremis; under torture or grief he will reach for "God" as exclamation, never as oath. He does not use slurs of any kind, has never been recorded doing so, and visibly winces when others do. He does not use the language of dominance — no "boy," no "kid" as condescension (only as affection), n
...[truncated]...
g given how much of it would apply to him. The vocabulary of self-examination has not been issued to him, and he has not requisitioned it.

---

## 6.2 REGISTER-SWITCHING ACROSS CONTEXT

The subject operates in at least **six discrete speech registers**, switched fluently and often within a single conversation:

**(a) Masked combat patter.** The densest, most performative register. Rapid-fire, joke-per-beat, structured in setup-punchline rhythm with frequent callbacks to earlier lines in the same fight. Vocabulary skews toward absurd similes ("you punch like my Aunt May after three espressos"), forced rhymes, mock-formality ("Mister Octopus, *if* I may"), and a recurring trick of **giving his opponents nicknames they did not ask for and which they cannot prevent from sticking** (Doc Ock, Shocker, Mysterio-as-fishbowl, Rhino-as-large-gentleman). The patter has two functions, both load-bearing: it regulates his own fear (rate of joke-production correlates inversely with felt safety — *the funnier he is, the more terrified he is*), and it operates as cognitive-load attack on opponents who expect a fighter, not a heckler. Surveillance recordings show measurable degradation in opponent reaction times after sustained Parker patter. He has weaponized annoyance.

**(b) Masked serious register.** When children are present, when civilians are dying, when a peer hero is in extremis, the patter cuts out cleanly and a different voice emerges — lower, slower, syntactically simpler, with second-person address ("hey, look at me, you're okay, look at me"). This register draws unmistakably on the cadence of a man calming a child, and almost certainly models Ben Parker's voice — it is the only register in which his diction loses all ornament. The shift is so reliable that experienced peers (Daredevil, Cage, Jessica Jones) read it as a tactical signal: if Spider-Man stops joking, the situation is worse than it looks.

**(c) Unmasked civilian neutral.** With strangers, photo-clients, Bugle staff, university colleagues. Polite, slightly self-deprecating, deferential to authority figures in a way that reads as habituated rather than felt. Frequent use of softeners ("sorry, um, would it be okay if —"), a verbal stammer that comes and goes with social pressure, and a tendency to volunteer unnecessary apology for taking up space. This register is **performatively smaller** than his actual presence; he has spent decades making himself easy to overlook in civilian frame, and the speech has been engineered to that end.

**(d) Unmasked intimate.** With May, with M

# COMMUNICATIONS & LINGUISTIC PROFILE — SUBJECT: MARY JANE WATSON

Classification: Longitudinal linguistic surveillance synthesis. Speech samples drawn from Lee/Romita introduction (ASM #42, 1966) through Wells run (present). Cross-referenced against alternate-continuity samples (Ultimate, Renew Your Vows, Spider-Girl flash-forwards) for stability of idiolect under different authorial hands.

---

## 1. BASELINE REGISTER — THE DEFAULT VOICE

Subject's default speaking voice is a mid-Atlantic American English overlaid with light residual Queens — the Queens surfaces only in two contexts: (a) emotional escalation, where dropped *g*'s and tightened vowels return ("nothin'," "talkin'," "ya kiddin' me"), and (b) deliberate code-switch downward when she wants to signal solidarity with a working-class interlocutor or undercut someone's pretension. Both are markers. The baseline she presents in neutral social settings is *acquired* — the speech of someone who consciously polished an accent to be castable, employable, and class-mobile. This is itself a linguistic tell: the standard register is the costume; the borough is the body underneath.

Speech rate is fast — measurably faster than Peter Parker's, faster than most of her female peer set in the canon. She talks *over* silences rather than into them. Pauses are managed: she will fill a beat with a small physical sound (laugh, exhale, a hummed *mm*) rather than allow dead air, because dead air is where the room can turn on her. The few instances on panel where she allows a long silence are diagnostic of either (a) full safety (late-arc Peter, Aunt May after the secret-share) or (b) total shutdown (separation arc, post-stillbirth).

Pitch travels higher in performance-mode (party scenes, public events, first encounters) and drops noticeably when she is alone with a trusted intimate — by the JMS run this drop is overt and Peter notices it on panel. The "real" voice is lower, slower, and approximately a third less verbose per beat than the public voice.

---

## 2. THE PARTY-GIRL REGISTER — INTRODUCTION-ERA AND ITS HALF-LIFE

The Lee/Romita-period voice is its own register and never fully retires; it gets recruited under specific conditions for the rest of her life. Markers:

- **Tiger.** The single most overdetermined word in the file. First deployed at introduction ("Face it, tiger — you just hit the jackpot"), it persists for sixty publication-years as a *targeted-affection signal* used exclusively for Peter. She does not call other men *tiger*. The word does three jobs at once: it claims him, it diminishes the threat-content of an emotional moment with mock-predator playfulness, and it preserves plausible deniability — *I'm not being sincere, I'm being cute*. When she uses *tiger* in a serious sentence, the seriousness is being smuggled. Track frequency: high in courtship, dips during marriage-routine, spikes around reconciliations and goodbyes. Its appearance in a goodbye is a near-certain marker that the goodbye is not final in her own internal model.
- **Daddy-O, Buster, Sport, Mister, Webhead, Web-head, Hot Stuff.** A rotating arsenal of mock-diminutives, period-coded to the 60s/70s in origin but kept alive into modern continuity as deliberate retro-cute affect. They are *all* deflection-vocatives: she nicknames people to manage them.
- **Italicized exclamations of delight** — "*Wow*," "*Crazy*," "*Wild*," "*Far out*" in early canon; updated to "*God*," "*Jesus*," "*Wow, okay*" later. The function is constant: she is performing surprise/pleasure at a volume designed to fill the room.
- **Self-third-person.** "MJ doesn't do mornings." "MJ needs coffee." Used sparingly, always under mild stress, always to put narrative distance between the speaking-self and the experiencing-self. A subtle but consistent dissociative-flag in the corpus.
- **The trailing laugh.** Sentences in this register frequently end on a soft laugh that is not synced to actual humor — a verbal *period* rather than an emotional response. The trailing laugh is the most reliable marker that she is operating in defensive-performance mode and not in earnest. Its *absence* in dialogue with Peter is the single fastest indicator that she has dropped the costume.

---

## 3. THE WORKING VOICE — CAREER, BUSINESS, MANAGEMENT

When subject is operating professionally — modeling sets, audition rooms, the nightclub-ownership arcs, Stark Industries adjacency (post-OMD), the rare reporter-mode in Ultimate continuity — her register tightens dramatically. Markers:

- Sentence length shortens. Verb-forward syntax. She gives instructions in two-beat units: *Move the light. Try it again.*
- Diminutives drop almost entirely; vocatives become functional (first names, *sir*, *honey* as light-professional-lubricant rather than affection).
- She uses the imperative more frequently than any other character in her social orbit. She is comfortable telling people what to do, and it does not register to her as aggression — it registers as efficiency. This is *not* the party girl. This is the daughter of Madeline Watson running the household at twelve.
- Negotiation language is clean, declarative, and unhedged. She does not pad refusals ("I'd love to, but —"); she refuses ("No, that doesn't work for me"). The lack of hedging in professional contexts is the single largest regi

I found the character distinction that makes the scene work: Felicia is controlled and selective about what she reveals; Wade’s chaos is partly camouflage; Peter can manage a supervillain faster than he can accept uncomplicated affection. And their Thursday friendship has to remain a real relationship in its own right, not just a scheme to win Peter.

The episode’s shape is a shopping-day comedy that gets hijacked by a proper Marvel villain. Felicia will tell us only what she sees, suspects, and eventually admits.

bash -lc cat > /mnt/data/Earth_GK_The_Thursday_Clause_master.txt <<'STORY'
# EARTH-GK: THE THURSDAY CLAUSE
## A Grizzly Knights story, narrated by Felicia Hardy

### 01. Eleven-Thirty Means Eleven-Thirty

@FELICIA.NARRATION [warm, amused, unhurried]
At eleven-thirty on a Thursday morning, Wade Wilson was halfway through his second cocktail at a Cheesecake Factory in Midtown, wearing sunglasses that cost more than the restaurant's espresso machine and explaining why Peter Parker needed a boyfriend.

@WADE [confidential, deadly serious]
"Not a boyfriend instead of a girlfriend. That is a false binary, and I am a complicated modern gentleman. I mean a boyfriend in addition to the six thousand other things the man thinks he has to be."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dry]
I held up my glass. The ice had surrendered. So had the first drink. Across from me sat a six-foot disaster with regenerative powers, a shopping list in twelve colors, and a peach-colored silk scarf tied around his neck like a very successful divorce.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [mildly entertained]
"He needs six consecutive hours of sleep, sugar. Then maybe we can address the boyfriend shortage."

@WADE [offended]
"First of all, I've offered to tuck him in. Secondly, I sent him a weighted blanket. Thirdly, he returned it because he thought there was a tracking device sewn into it."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [cool]
"Was there?"

@WADE [after a beat, sheepish]
"A tasteful one."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [fond, hidden under the polish]
This was our seventh year of Thursdays. Seven years of long lunches, expensive mistakes, and shopping trips that began with exquisite intentions and ended with one of us carrying something that the authorities would describe as evidence. We had rules. No costumes. No jobs. No killing anyone in a retail establishment. Absolutely no trying to fix Peter Parker before dessert.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [lower, intimate]
The friendship was secret. Not because I was ashamed of Wade. Not because he was ashamed of me. We had simply discovered a little corner of Manhattan where no one needed either of us to perform our job description. He didn't need to be the joke. I didn't need to be the cat. We could eat too much, drink something with a ridiculous name, and discuss a man neither of us had the slightest intention of possessing.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dry]
Not that possession had ever been our strongest subject.

@WADE [bright, counting on fingers]
"Peter likes you. Peter likes me. Peter likes MJ. Peter once looked at Daredevil like the sight of a man in red leather had personally rearranged his chromosomes. The answer is right in front of us."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [warning wrapped in velvet]
"Wade."

@WADE [quickly]
"I am not diagnosing him. I am arranging a seating chart. That boy has a heart with six emergency exits and no idea where the front door is."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [thoughtful]
That was unpleasantly close to true. Peter was capable of wanting men, women, more than one person, and probably the concept of a sincere compliment if he could approach it under cover of darkness. He was also capable of turning every desire into a hearing before the Supreme Court of His Own Guilt. Labels were less interesting to me than behavior. I had watched the behavior for years. He would drag a stranger out of a collapsing building without checking the odds, then spend forty minutes trying to determine whether accepting a ride home imposed an unfair burden on the driver.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [casual]
"You could simply tell him you like him."

@WADE [horrified whisper]
"And risk a conversation? In person? You monster."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [smiling]
Marisol appeared beside our table carrying a plate of fries neither of us had ordered. She had been our server on Thursdays for the better part of three years. Wade tipped extravagantly, never touched her without permission, and once threatened a man who snapped his fingers at her. Marisol tolerated the rest of him as a professional accommodation.

@MARISOL [brisk, familiar]
"One of you has to eat something with actual nutritional value. And no, Mr. Wilson, the strawberry on your drink doesn't count."

@WADE [deeply moved]
"You see me, Marisol. You see the wounded boy beneath the designer sunglasses."

@MARISOL [without missing a beat]
"I see a man about to order his third drink. Water first."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dry]
She placed two glasses of water on the table. He saluted. I gave her the kind of smile normally reserved for rare jewels and women who know the difference between a request and a command.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [subtle shift to alertness]
Then Peter Parker walked into the restaurant.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [measured]
Except he didn't.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [precise, observational]
The man had Peter's face, Peter's shoulders, and a very convincing approximation of Peter's tired little smile. His shirt was clean. His shoes were expensive. His posture suggested a person who had slept indoors for eight hours and had never once apologized to a coffee table after walking into it.

@WADE [under his breath]
"Oh, look. Someone finally put him in the washing machine."

@FAKE_PETER [gentle, rehearsed]
"Felicia. Wade. I was hoping to find you together."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dryly suspicious]
That was interesting. No one was supposed to find us together. I let my eyes travel to the shopping bag in his hand, the gold watch on his wrist, and the door he had left open behind him. A small detail. Peter always closed doors. He'd once doubled back across a burning laboratory to shut one because it was letting smoke into a stairwell.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [light, testing]
"Sweetheart. What a surprise."

@FAKE_PETER [warm, too smooth]
"I need your help. Both of you."

@WADE [suddenly delighted]
"Oh, this is already the best Thursday I've ever had. Do we get matching rings?"

@FAKE_PETER [smiling]
"I was thinking a shopping trip."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a thin edge]
There are plenty of ways to counterfeit a face. There is only one Peter Parker, and he had never, in the entire time I'd known him, initiated a shopping trip without a medical emergency, a wedding, or the imminent collapse of civilization.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [soft, baiting]
"What do you need us to buy, Spider?"

@FAKE_PETER [a fraction too eager]
"Not buy. Retrieve. Something dangerous is being held at Vesper and Vale, two blocks over. We can keep it out of the wrong hands if you open their private collection room."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [cool]
He slid a cream envelope over the table. On the front was a tiny embossed silver spider. The invitation to a high-end criminal transaction had apparently been designed by somebody's wedding planner.

@WADE [proud]
"I made a mood board like this once. It was for my own funeral."

@FAKE_PETER [pressing]
"It's important. People could get hurt."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quietly hard]
There. The lever. People could get hurt. An experienced manipulator could move the real Peter around like a chess piece with those four words. This man had made the mistake of believing the rest of us were the same kind of fool.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [playful]
"Of course, darling. Give us ten minutes."

@FAKE_PETER [relieved]
"Thank you. You two are amazing."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sharp, private]
He left without asking what our drinks were, whether either of us had eaten, or if the restaurant would mind him borrowing two of its customers. The genuine article would have worried about every one of those things and somehow apologized for a fourth that hadn't happened.

@WADE [barely audible]
"Not him."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [low]
"No."

@WADE [with an edge beneath the joke]
"Can we shoot the face-stealing impostor?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [pleasant]
"After dessert, if he's earned it."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [private, suddenly serious]
My phone lit up. A message from Peter, the actual one. He had sent Wade a photograph of a six-foot chocolate statue with the caption: WHY IS THIS OUTSIDE MY APARTMENT?

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dry]
Wade had definitely earned it. But our new friend had earned my attention, and I was about to discover whether he'd earned something worse.

### 02. The Rules of a Good Heist

@FELICIA.NARRATION [brightening, sophisticated]
Vesper and Vale was the kind of Midtown boutique where the lighting made every customer look wealthy and every piece of merchandise look ashamed of its price tag. I liked it immediately. The front window held a necklace with a diamond so badly cut I wanted to find the jeweler and offer condolences to the stone.

@WADE [genuinely distressed]
"They have a leather jacket that costs fourteen thousand dollars. Is it made from the original cow? Does the cow still get visiting rights?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [matter of fact]
"That particular leather comes from a designer who hates poor people. Feel free to steal it."

@WADE [a scandalized whisper]
"Felicia. On a Thursday?"

@FELICIA.NARRATION [smiling]
Wade had bought an emerald-green scarf on the way over, then put it on a bronze statue outside the store. I had taken it back. The statue lacked the bone structure, and Wade had sentimental ideas about public art.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [watchful]
Our false Peter stood near a private elevator, talking to a sales director whose smile didn't reach her eyes. I stayed near the cases and pretended to consider a bracelet. Wade tested the flexibility of a coat that looked like it belonged to a particularly rich vampire.

@FAKE_PETER [confident]
"The private collection. Felicia has authorization."

@SALES_DIRECTOR [hesitant, formal]
"Ms. Hardy's authorization was revoked six years ago, sir."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [delighted]
That was a lovely surprise. Apparently they remembered the St. Petersburg tiara. I'd put it back, eventually, after teaching its owner several things about provenance and security.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [smiling at the director]
"Mrs. Vale. How wonderful you kept the records."

@SALES_DIRECTOR [controlled irritation]
"It's Ms. Vale. And you returned the tiara without the original clasp."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [airily]
"The clasp was a counterfeit. Consider it a consultation fee."

@WADE [aside, impressed]
"This is pornography for people who own lockpicks."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [precise]
The security door bore a new keypad and two biometric sensors. Our impersonator hadn't brought us because he loved our company. He needed someone to get past a lock he'd been told I could open. The little silver-spider invitation was a flourish. The desperate pitch had been the real key.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [innocent]
"And what exactly is in there, Peter?"

@FAKE_PETER [quickly]
"An item called the Asterion. A compact resonance assembly. It can interfere with enhanced sensory systems."

@WADE [to Felicia, brightly]
"A noise-canceling headphone for our favorite insomnia enthusiast."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a chill]
My smile stayed where it was. I knew a good deal about the machinery criminals bought when they wanted to hurt Spider-Man. Adhesives. Sonic cannons. Pressurized gases. Every few years someone decided the problem was the precognitive warning that lived just behind his eyes. Someone had apparently made a smaller, prettier solution.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [pleasant, to the impostor]
"If it's that dangerous, why didn't you bring Reed?"

@FAKE_PETER [reassuring]
"I don't want to involve the Fantastic Four."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dry]
The real Peter had once involved the Fantastic Four because his toaster was making a noise he couldn't reproduce. The impostor was not improving.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [softly]
"What do you want, darling?"

@FAKE_PETER [stepping closer]
"I want us to work together again. I miss you."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [temperature drops]
He reached for my hand. I stepped back. A stranger borrowing a loved one's face is already trespassing. Using that face to purchase consent is a different kind of theft.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [cold]
"No."

@FAKE_PETER [the warmth evaporates]
"You misunderstand."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [level]
"I understand you perfectly. You should run."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quick]
He did. His face shimmered in the reflection of a display case, the nose flattening, the eyes narrowing. Dmitri Smerdyakov. The Chameleon. I had never liked a man whose special talent was making the people around him doubt their own eyes.

@WADE [happy, weaponized]
"Oh, wonderful. A work acquaintance. Dmitri, you look like a clearance rack trying to be a person."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sharp]
Chameleon pressed a button under his watch. Steel shutters dropped over the boutique's windows. The elevator lit red. And from the ceiling came a voice with the familiar flat irritation of a man who had built his whole personality around the fact that his gloves were louder than his jokes.

@SHOCKER [tinny over speaker]
"Nobody moves. This is a professional operation."

@WADE [calling upward]
"Herman? You got promoted to ceiling?"

@SHOCKER [enraged]
"My name is SHOCKER!"

@WADE [thoughtful]
"Your mom must have had a terrible obstetrician."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [tight]
The sales director dropped to one knee. Three customers froze beside the displays. A young stock clerk stood near the locked back door, white-knuckled around a clipboard. Chameleon was gone, but the trap had him written all over it. Our secret Thursday hadn't brought us to the crime. The crime had been brought to Thursday.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [decisive]
And as it turned out, nobody had asked my permission.

### 03. The Boy in the Window

@FELICIA.NARRATION [faster, still controlled]
I had three options. Open the vault. Break the shutters. Or persuade Wade not to turn the jewelry department into a war crime. The third required immediate attention.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [quiet command]
"No guns until we know where everyone is."

@WADE [offended, but complies]
"I know. Thursday rule. Also, I am deeply wounded that you think I'd endanger the civilians."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [affectionate, honest]
He wasn't lying. Wade joked about murder with the cheerful fluency other men reserved for baseball. He also paid attention to where frightened people stood. He had already stepped between the sales director and the sound coming from upstairs, putting his own broad, scarred body where somebody else might have put a display stand.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [alert]
My earpiece wasn't supposed to be with me. It was. I've always had a complicated relationship with rules I wrote myself.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [low, into earpiece]
"Bruno. If my signal disappears, call Reed Richards and tell him the Asterion is loose."

@BRUNO [sleepy, irritated]
"It's eleven forty-eight. What happened to your day off?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [crisp]
"Someone bought it without asking."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [an eyebrow in her voice]
A crash came from above. Glass dust sifted down across the display cases. Spider-Man landed in a crouch on top of a velvet bench, three seconds too late to make an entrance and fifty seconds ahead of a catastrophe.

@PETER [fast, reflexive patter]
"Good morning, retail criminals! I was just in the neighborhood doing a routine skyscraper inspection, and wow, these prices are criminal even by actual-crime standards."

@WADE [giddy]
"There he is. My emotional support arachnid."

@PETER [a beat, alarmed]
"Wade? Felicia? Together? In a boutique? On a Thursday?"

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dry]
He had the good sense to look more frightened by that than by the shutters.

@PETER [rapid]
"Are you both okay? How many people? Why is there a dampening signal in here? Why am I asking the questions in that order? Are you hurt?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [cutting through]
"Six civilians that I can see. Chameleon escaped into the service corridor. Shocker is upstairs. The dampener is in the private collection room. Breathe, Spider."

@PETER [automatic]
"I'm breathing. Well, technically I'm hyperventilating, but in a way that respects the definition of breathing."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [private]
He wasn't looking at me anymore. He was counting exits, reflections, hands. He didn't have to say the rest of his arithmetic aloud. Every new number became a person he might fail.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sudden motion]
A second crash shook the door. Johnny Storm drifted in through the broken upper window with all the tact of a fireworks display entering a library.

@JOHNNY [gloriously pleased]
"Which one of you called about stolen Baxter tech? I was ten minutes from a lunch date."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [eyes on the ceiling]
"Nobody called you."

@JOHNNY [offhand]
"Reed did. Apparently somebody borrowed our resonance stack without the courtesy of setting off the usual seventeen alarms."

@PETER [distracted]
"Reed knows? Great. That's actually really great. The resonant frequency is—"

@JOHNNY [interrupting]
"He said don't touch it, don't heat it, don't explain it to anybody. Especially you."

@WADE [solemnly]
"The man has read our relationship history."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [gently vicious]
A hum began under the floor. Peter went perfectly still. It was worse than watching him wince. He lifted one hand slowly, like a man reaching for a light switch in a house he'd known since childhood and finding an unfamiliar wall.

@PETER [quiet, frightened]
"My spider-sense just stopped."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [plain, no joke]
The room hadn't become safe. The warning had disappeared. Those are not the same thing. Peter's eyes moved to the shutters, then the clerk, then the skylight. I knew what came next. He would throw himself at the largest threat on the theory that he could absorb the damage faster than anyone else.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [firm]
"Peter. Don't move."

@PETER [too fast]
"I can get through the upper mezzanine. If I hit the source before it charges again—"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [cold, absolute]
"No."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a little quieter]
He looked at me. I almost never used his name like that. It meant I wasn't flirting, and I wasn't going anywhere.

@WADE [low, sincere]
"P, I can take the first hit. That's not me auditioning for martyr of the month. That's my actual stupid superpower. Let us work."

@PETER [struggling]
"I don't want you to have to—"

@WADE [gently, with effort]
"I know. You don't want anyone to have to anything. That is why we have a team and why you are not allowed to be all of it."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [controlled inhale]
Then a woman's voice cut across the boutique from the locked side entrance.

@MJ [authoritative]
"Hello? If someone has turned my wardrobe pickup into an armed robbery, I'm billing them for the entire shoot."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [wry relief]
Mary Jane Watson had arrived. The universe was about to meet its supervisor.

### 04. The Girl Who Always Sees the Room

@FELICIA.NARRATION [measured, respectful]
MJ was on the other side of the security glass with a garment bag over one shoulder and a pair of heels in her hand. The shutters had trapped us inside and her outside. She wasn't asking to be rescued. She was looking for the control panel.

@MJ [fast, crisp]
"Felicia. How many?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [focused]
"Six civilians, four of us, one extremely loud villain, and Chameleon somewhere in the building."

@MJ [already processing]
"The stockroom on my side connects to the service elevators. I know the manager. I'll get the doors open. Don't send anyone toward the second floor. There are rigging lines across that corridor for the installation."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [appreciative]
She had mapped a route while the rest of the room was still discussing the crisis. People mistook beauty for the absence of attention. It was an error Mary Jane encouraged right up until it became useful to her.

@WADE [calling through the glass]
"MJ! Question, for the record. Did you know Felicia and I are secret Thursday besties?"

@MJ [without looking up]
"You bought me birthday shoes every year with the same lunch receipt in the bag. Yes."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [disgusted affection]
Wade looked betrayed. I looked at him. He shrugged. The man had never once successfully hidden a gift, a grenade, or a romantic feeling.

@JOHNNY [amused]
"Wait. You two go shopping together? That is adorable."

@WADE [offended]
"This is a private, sophisticated friendship."

@JOHNNY [to Felicia]
"He has glitter on his collar."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [flat]
"That's why it's private."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [edge rises]
The hum became a vibration in my teeth. Overhead, the light fixtures shivered. A series of low, brutal blows struck the private elevator door. Shocker was trying to break through from inside. The private collection room held whatever he'd come to extract. He had misjudged the security. So had our fake Peter.

@PETER [fast, technical]
"If the assembly hits resonance, it won't just take out my warning system. It'll shake the support connections apart. The floor might lose its anchors."

@JOHNNY [practical]
"I can melt the emitter."

@PETER [immediate]
"No. The waveguide is probably ceramic. Rapid thermal expansion could turn it into shrapnel."

@JOHNNY [grudging]
"Fine. You get one nerd speech."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [thoughtful]
I was watching the sales director. Her hand had stayed on the same ring finger since the shutters dropped. A small habit, but I recognized it. People who know a vault's emergency release tend to touch the location where they remember it being. Hers was hidden beneath the counter.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [to director, calm]
"Ms. Vale. The emergency release. Where?"

@SALES_DIRECTOR [shaken]
"It's a manual key under the display. But if you turn it, the collection lock loses power and the service shaft opens."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [decisive]
"Precisely. Thank you. Wade, stay with the customers. Johnny, hold the upper opening without bringing the whole building down. Peter, take the clerk and get everyone behind the double doors. Let me handle the lock."

@PETER [hesitating]
"If it's wired to the emitter—"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [gentle, edged]
"Then I'll know before you do. It's my profession. I trust you with people. Trust me with the lock."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [soft]
There it was. The smallest possible pause, the most expensive choice he knew how to make. Someone else was going to do a dangerous thing while he stood where he'd been asked to stand. His shoulders tightened. Then he nodded.

@PETER [low, committed]
"Okay. Six people. Two doors. I've got them."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [relieved]
It wasn't a cure. It was a job assignment. Sometimes that was plenty.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sudden]
The private elevator exploded outward. Shocker came through in yellow armor, his gauntlets barking against the tile. Johnny caught the first shockwave above the customers. Peter threw an entire wall of webbing across the space between them. Wade advanced into the noise, smiling in the way that usually preceded both excellent jokes and terrible medical bills.

@SHOCKER [furious]
"Everybody DOWN!"

@WADE [cheerfully dangerous]
"Herman, we've talked about inside voices."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sharp, fleet]
I slid beneath the display counter. There it was: a keyhole no bigger than a pencil eraser, deliberately hidden behind a hinged strip of walnut. Most people think locks are questions. A good thief knows they're an invitation to learn what the builder was afraid of.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [confident]
This builder had been afraid of me. Delicious.

### 05. A Very Expensive Place to Bleed

@FELICIA.NARRATION [tight, kinetic]
The key turned half a degree and stopped. Wrong tension. The emergency mechanism was wired to a secondary catch, newly installed, far too neat for Ms. Vale's security contractor. Chameleon had prepared for me. I smiled despite myself. Men with elaborate plans often mistake preparedness for imagination.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [observant]
Above the counter, Wade took a shockwave through the chest and hit a mannequin hard enough to send its head rolling across the floor. He sat up amid twelve thousand dollars of shattered tailoring.

@WADE [breathless, indignant]
"That mannequin was innocent! He had a wife! She was made of polyurethane, but they were happy!"

@SHOCKER [straining]
"Why don't you STAY DOWN?"

@WADE [suddenly cold]
"Because the people behind me don't get back up."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [low]
He said it without a joke. Shocker heard the difference. So did I. Deadpool is a name people put on a man to avoid looking too closely at what he survives. Some days he encouraged it. On that Thursday he simply held the line.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sharpening]
My pick caught the second spring. The display unlocked. Across the store, MJ swung the side security shutter open just wide enough for two adults at a time. Peter had woven a protected corridor down the center aisle, gentle little lanes of webbing that kept the customers away from the sound of Shocker's gauntlets.

@MJ [commanding, warm]
"Come to my voice. Don't run. You've got a clear path. Keep your eyes on me, not on the fight."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [private, admiring]
I watched the stock clerk take her hand. I watched the sales director help an older customer over the broken tile. Peter held the corridor like a bridge. The world asked him to save people so often he had almost forgotten that other people sometimes helped.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [tension]
My wrist brushed the edge of the private vault. A small black module was mounted beneath a gold lattice, ringed with blue ceramic. The Asterion. It didn't look like much. Evil engineering rarely does. It simply needed to work once, on the right target.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quick]
I reached for it. A woman's voice spoke behind me. Mine.

@CHAMELEON_AS_FELICIA [silken, artificial]
"Careful, darling. The real thing bites."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [cold amusement]
Chameleon stood six feet away wearing my face and a duplicate of my white blouse, having apparently decided the second-best way to rob me was to become me.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [softly disgusted]
"Dmitri. You've copied the silk. You haven't copied the tailoring."

@CHAMELEON_AS_FELICIA [mocking]
"The key, Felicia. I don't need to fight you. I just need your hands."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [flat]
He pointed a small handgun at my chest. Nothing ornate. No theatrics. A rather disappointing choice for a man so committed to costume.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [almost bored]
"If you're going to wear my face, at least buy a better gun."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [sudden]
He fired. I was already moving. The bullet took a display lamp, and the shower of glass knocked the weapon aside. I caught his wrist, used the counter as a fulcrum, and folded him into a position that would make his orthopedic surgeon question his profession.

@CHAMELEON_AS_FELICIA [pained]
"You are making a mistake."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [quiet]
"I've made prettier ones."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [urgent]
The Asterion began to pulse. There was a deep, ugly sound, not quite audible, more like a migraine developing in the bones of the room. Peter dropped one knee. The webbing shook. Shocker realized his gauntlets were feeding the resonance instead of controlling it, and his expression changed from aggression to disbelief.

@SHOCKER [panicked]
"That's not supposed to happen."

@JOHNNY [shouting]
"Pete! The ceiling anchors!"

@PETER [strained, serious]
"I've got the supports. Get them out."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [fast]
I didn't have time to disassemble the Asterion. I had time to steal it. That is a different skill, and I'm much better at the second.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [concentrated]
Three pins held the ceramic ring. I lifted the first. The second was pressure-balanced. The third was wired to a microscopic switch. I slid one of Wade's absurdly expensive silver cuff links beneath it and let the mechanism settle onto the substitute weight. The vibration lost its edge. One clean movement and the Asterion was in my palm.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [through teeth]
"Johnny. Outside. Up and away from people. I've got the emitter."

@JOHNNY [focused]
"Copy."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a rush]
He punched upward through the ruined skylight trailing a ribbon of controlled fire. A blue flash went with him, then a hard crack from somewhere above the roofline. The pressure vanished. I knew better than to ask a man who'd just saved our lives how he planned to explain that to his sister.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [slower, dread underneath]
The webbing held. The lights stopped shaking. One frightened clerk began to laugh, that awful involuntary sound people make when their bodies decide danger is over before their minds agree.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quiet]
Then I saw Peter.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [close]
He had caught Shocker before Herman could fall into the broken elevator shaft. Of course he had. The villain would walk away in restraints and the boy who saved him was bleeding through the shoulder of his suit, looking around for somebody else he might have missed.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [gritted, tenderness suppressed]
I wanted to kiss him. I wanted to slap him. Neither would have fixed the wound. I settled for walking over with the most expensive piece of evidence in Manhattan in my hand.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [soft]
"Spider. Everybody's out."

@PETER [immediate, reflex]
"Did anyone get hurt?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [precise]
"Yes. You. Wade. A security guard has a cut above his eye. The rest are upright. Nobody died. You don't have to turn that into a failure."

@PETER [unsteady]
"I almost—"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [no room for it]
"I know. That's why the rest of us were here."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [still]
He looked at me, then toward Wade, who was trying to straighten a mannequin's severed head, and finally toward MJ at the exit. He didn't apologize. I considered it the rarest event of the afternoon.

### 06. Five People and One Fitting Room

@FELICIA.NARRATION [softly comic]
By two-forty, Shocker was in custody, Chameleon was secured in a restraint designed for a man with no honest face, and Vesper and Vale had decided insurance fraud was easier than explaining superhero damage. Johnny emerged from the roof looking smug and faintly singed.

@JOHNNY [trying to sound casual]
"Reed wants the emitter. Sue wants a damage report. Ben wants me to tell all of you that I owe him twenty bucks for betting I'd set a suit on fire."

@WADE [reverent]
"I like Ben."

@JOHNNY [deadpan]
"Everybody likes Ben. You only stop when he likes you back."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [private]
Johnny looked at Peter's shoulder, then at MJ. He made a smart choice. He left without announcing he'd made it. I have always liked that about him more than his publicist would approve of.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [slow]
The rest of us ended up in a private fitting room because it was the only place in the boutique with a chair, an intact mirror, and nobody expecting a press statement. Wade had borrowed a crimson jacket with a price tag that looked like the GDP of a small principality. Peter sat on a padded bench trying to pretend his shoulder didn't hurt. MJ leaned against the wall, heels in one hand, gaze moving steadily between him and us.

@MJ [firm, familiar]
"Peter. Let me see the shoulder."

@PETER [automatic]
"It's fine."

@MJ [flat]
"That's not an answer."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [dryly affectionate]
He let her look. Some people believe affection is a feeling. In our particular little disaster zone, it was often a consent negotiation conducted over a bloodstain.

@WADE [bright, but oddly nervous]
"Okay. While we're doing terrifying conversations, I'd like to make a statement."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a quick glance]
I recognized that register. Wade used it when he was about to pretend the truth was a joke, and when he knew I'd notice.

@WADE [with effort, sincere]
"Peter. I have a crush on you. A real one. Not a bit. Not a dare. Not an invitation you owe me an answer to. I like the way you explain things when you're excited, even though I understand one word in nine. I like that you stopped a fight once to make sure a guy who was trying to kill you had his inhaler. I even like the stupid way you apologize to chairs. I like you."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [small, honest]
No one said anything. For Wade, silence was an act of physical courage. He'd stood in front of Shocker's gauntlets with less visible effort.

@PETER [barely above a whisper]
"Wade."

@WADE [quickly, trying humor as oxygen]
"I know, it's weird. The jacket isn't helping. But I thought you deserved one adult telling you a thing without making it a hostage situation."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quiet]
Peter looked at his hands. He had the expression he wore when a rescue was almost complete and he was waiting for the ceiling to come down. There were people who could diagnose that expression until they ran out of labels. I knew the more practical fact. When someone wanted him, his first instinct was to make sure they hadn't made a mistake.

@PETER [uncertain, honest]
"I didn't think you'd want me to know."

@WADE [soft]
"I did. I was just scared."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a small exhale]
I could steal a jewel from a vault with four cameras, two guards, and a laser grid. Saying an ordinary true thing to a man sitting six feet away suddenly seemed like an insultingly difficult assignment.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [plain, no pet-name]
"Peter. I still want you. That has never been the part I was confused about. I don't want you to make a promise because you think my happiness is your responsibility. And I don't want you treating Wade like a punchline because it would be easier than taking him seriously."

@PETER [urgent, clear]
"I wouldn't."

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [gentler]
"I know. I'm telling you what I need, not accusing you of what you've done."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [watchful]
MJ regarded me with that unnerving clarity of hers. Once, we had treated our different histories with Peter like competing legal claims. It took us years to understand that neither of us wanted to win a person.

@MJ [measured, direct]
"And I'm going to say something, too. I love Peter. That's not a standing agreement to whatever arrangement somebody invents while we're bleeding in a dressing room. If we ever talk about changing anything, we talk about it separately, clearly, and with every person free to say no. Including me."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [respectful]
That was why I liked her. Mary Jane knew exactly where a door was, and she didn't apologize for keeping the key.

@PETER [after a long beat]
"You're right. All three of you."

@WADE [in a whisper]
"Holy shit. A sentence I can frame."

@PETER [a nervous laugh, then real]
"I have thought about it. More than one person. Not just in some abstract, hypothetical, 'wow that would be complicated' way. I've been attracted to men. I've been attracted to women. I've wanted people at the same time, and then felt like wanting was already a kind of betrayal."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [softly]
He looked straight at Wade. Poor boy. He could hang off the side of the Empire State Building by one finger, but this was what courage looked like up close.

@PETER [honest, still trembling]
"I've thought about you, Wade. And Felicia, you know I have. I don't know what the right shape of any of this is. I know I can't keep making my feelings everybody else's problem by refusing to name them."

@WADE [awed, almost inaudible]
"You thought about me?"

@PETER [a tiny smile]
"You wore a tuxedo to help me move a sofa. You looked really good."

@WADE [shattered with joy]
"That was a rented tuxedo. I want that on my tombstone."

@MJ [practical, fond]
"Nobody is deciding the future today. Especially not in clothes we haven't paid for. Peter, you're going to get that shoulder checked. Wade, the jacket goes back on its hanger. Felicia, stop pretending you aren't holding evidence in your handbag."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [offended delight]
I had hidden the Asterion inside the lining of my purse. She hadn't seen me do it. That, at least, was what I was going to tell myself.

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [smiling]
"You take the romance out of everything, darling."

@MJ [with a smile of her own]
"No. I just make sure it survives contact with reality."

### 07. The Thursday Clause

@FELICIA.NARRATION [easy, reflective]
I delivered the Asterion to Reed through a discreet intermediary, then sent Ms. Vale a check for the security guard's medical expenses and an anonymous note about the counterfeit stone in her front window. The check was legitimate. The note was gratuitous. No one's perfect.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quiet]
Peter went to a doctor. I know because I made him send a photograph of the discharge instructions, and because MJ confirmed he hadn't taken the picture from the internet. Wade spent the afternoon shopping for a frame for Peter's accidental compliment. He bought three. One was shaped like a heart. One had a tiny plastic Spider-Man dangling from the corner. The third was, inexplicably, a taxidermied-looking squirrel.

@WADE [remembered, appalled]
"It's an artisanal squirrel. Have some culture."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [fond]
There are kinds of devotion you don't recognize until someone sets them down on the table without demanding interest. Our Thursdays had survived jealous people, paid killers, unpaid parking tickets, a dimensional incursion on Fifth Avenue, and the time Wade tried to buy a live peacock because the color reminded him of a pair of my shoes. This was the first Thursday that had actually frightened us.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [a beat]
Not because of the gun. Not because of the emitter. Because we had finally said the things that couldn't be stolen back.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [wry]
The following Thursday, at eleven-thirty, Wade and I were back at our booth at the Cheesecake Factory in Midtown. He wore a pink suit. I wore black. Between us sat a basket of bread, two drinks, and a shopping list that had been amended to include the words NO BODY ARMOR IN THE CASHMERE STORE.

@MARISOL [dry, familiar]
"The usual?"

@WADE [delighted]
"The usual. And a very large slice of cheesecake. We've had personal growth. It's disgusting."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [small smile]
My phone buzzed. Peter had sent a message. Not an emergency. Not an apology. A photograph of his breakfast, followed by four words: I am taking today off.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [an intimate pause]
Wade looked at the screen and held his breath. I had seen him swallow bullets. It was harder for him to believe the boy might have swallowed a decent meal.

@WADE [hushed]
"Did we do that?"

@FELICIA.DIALOGUE [soft, careful]
"No, sugar. He did."

@FELICIA.NARRATION [quiet pride]
I put the phone away. There were things I stole for money, things I stole for beauty, and a few precious things I kept because they had never been for sale.

@FELICIA.NARRATION [smiling, final]
Thursdays belonged to the last category. And Wade still had absolutely terrible taste in scarves.

The story is written now, and the first-person rule holds through all seven chapters. One deliberate choice: Peter’s attraction to more than one person is part of his Earth-GK characterization, but the scene won’t turn a clinical label into a punchline or assume that MJ has consented to an arrangement. Wade gets to say something sincere without having it dismissed as another joke.

I’m converting it into the readable EPUB and a line-by-line cast script, then making a clearly labeled local audio audition rather than pretending it came from ElevenLabs.
